\displaystyle\mathrm{P}(|\Theta_{2}|\geq\epsilon\omega)\leq n\exp\bigg\{-C_{\epsilon}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}{\mathrm{tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{22.1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{21}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{11}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{12})}}\bigg\}\cdot\mathrm{log}n\rightarrow 0,

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}(|\Theta_{3}|\geq\epsilon\omega)\leq n\exp\bigg\{-C_{\epsilon}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}{\mathrm{tr}[({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{21}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{11}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{12})^{2}]}}\bigg\}\cdot\mathrm{log}n\rightarrow 0.

8.6.2
non-Gaussian sequence

For
\boldsymbol{z}=(z_{1},\dots,z_{q})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}
, we consider a smooth approximation of the maximum function
\boldsymbol{z}\rightarrow\max\limits_{1\leq i\leq q}z_{i}
, namely,

\displaystyle F_{\beta}(\boldsymbol{z})=\beta^{-1}\mathrm{log}\bigg\{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{q}\exp(\beta z_{j})\bigg\},

where
\beta>0
is the smoothing parameter that controls the level of approximation. An elementary calculation shows that
\forall\boldsymbol{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}
,

\displaystyle 0\leq F_{\beta}(\boldsymbol{z})-\max\limits_{1\leq j\leq q}z_{j}\leq\beta^{-1}\mathrm{log}q,

see
Chernozhukov et al. 2013
.

For any
i=1,\dots,n
and
k=1,\dots,n-1
, we define

\displaystyle\breve{a}_{i,k}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}1-\dfrac{k}{n},&i\leq k,\\
-\dfrac{k}{n},&i>k,\end{array}\right.

and reformulate

\displaystyle C_{\gamma,j}(k)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigg\{\frac{k}{n}\bigg(1-\frac{k}{n}\bigg)\bigg\}^{-\gamma}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{k}X_{ij}-\frac{k}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}X_{ij}\bigg)/\hat{\sigma}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg\{\frac{k}{n}\bigg(1-\frac{k}{n}\bigg)\bigg\}^{-\gamma}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\breve{a}_{i,k}X_{ij}/\hat{\sigma}_{j},

and

\displaystyle W(k)-\mu_{M,k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n\sqrt{p}}\sum\limits_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\breve{a}_{i,k}\breve{a}_{j,k}\boldsymbol{X}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{j}+o_{p}(\omega).

with the analysis in Theorem 1.

Define

\displaystyle U(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{n})

\displaystyle:=\beta^{-1}\mathrm{log}\bigg\{\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n-1}\exp\bigg(2\beta n^{-1}\sum\limits_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\breve{a}_{i,k}\breve{a}_{j,k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{j}/\sqrt{2\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}\bigg)\bigg\},

\displaystyle V(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{n})

\displaystyle:=\beta^{-1}\mathrm{log}\bigg\{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n-1}\exp\bigg(\beta n^{-1/2}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\breve{a}_{i,k}\epsilon_{ij}\bigg)\bigg\},

by letting
\beta=n^{1/8}\mathrm{log}(np)
, it is suffices to prove

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}(U(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{n})\leq x,V(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{n})\leq u_{p}\{\exp(-y)\}\rightarrow F_{V}(x)\cdot\exp\{-\exp(-y)\}.

According to the results of Section
8.6.1
, it remains to show that

(U(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{n}),V(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{n}))
has the same limiting distribution as
(U({\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{1},\dots,{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{n}),V({\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{1},\dots,{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{n}))
.
Similar to the proof of Lemma
8.2
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{f(U(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{n}),V(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{n}))\}-\mathbb{E}\{f(U({\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{1},\dots,{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{n}),V({\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{1},\dots,{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{n}))\}\rightarrow 0,

for any
f\in\mathcal{C}_{b}^{3}(\mathbb{R})
as
(n,p)\rightarrow\infty
.

Furthermore, for
d=1,\dots,n+1
, we define

\displaystyle U_{d}:=U(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{d-1},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d},\dots,{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{n}),

\displaystyle U_{d,0}:=\beta^{-1}\mathrm{log}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\exp\left\{\beta n^{-1}\left(\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq d-1}\breve{a}_{i,k}\breve{a}_{j,k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{j}+\sum_{d+1\leq i<j\leq n}\breve{a}_{i,k}\breve{a}_{j,k}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{j}\right.\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\left.\left.+\sum_{i=1}^{d-1}\sum_{j=d+1}^{n}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}\breve{a}_{\ell_{2},k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{j}\right)/\sqrt{2\text{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle V_{d}:=V(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{d-1},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d},\dots,{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{n}),

\displaystyle V_{d,0}:=\beta^{-1}\mathrm{log}\bigg\{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n-1}\exp\bigg(\beta n^{-1/2}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{d-1}\breve{a}_{i,k}\epsilon_{ij}+\beta n^{-1/2}\sum\limits_{i=d+1}^{n}\breve{a}_{i,k}\xi_{ij}\bigg)\bigg\}.

Then,

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}\{f(U(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{n}),V(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{n}))\}-\mathbb{E}\{f(U({\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{1},\dots,{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{n}),V({\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{1},\dots,{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{n}))\}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{d=1}^{n}|\mathbb{E}\{f(U_{d},V_{d})\}-\mathbb{E}\{f(U_{d+1},V_{d+1})\}|.

By Taylor’s expansion, we have

\displaystyle f(U_{d},V_{d})-f(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{1}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(U_{d}-U_{d,0})+f_{2}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(V_{d}-V_{d,0})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}f_{11}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(U_{d}-U_{d,0})^{2}+\frac{1}{2}f_{22}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(V_{d}-V_{d,0})^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}f_{12}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(U_{d}-U_{d,0})(V_{d}-V_{d,0})+O(|U_{d}-U_{d,0}|^{3})+O(|V_{d}-V_{d,0}|^{3}),

and

\displaystyle f(U_{d+1},V_{d+1})-f(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{1}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(U_{d+1}-U_{d,0})+f_{2}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(V_{d+1}-V_{d,0})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}f_{11}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(U_{d+1}-U_{d,0})^{2}+\frac{1}{2}f_{22}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(V_{d+1}-V_{d,0})^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}f_{12}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(U_{d+1}-U_{d,0})(V_{d+1}-V_{d,0})+O(|U_{d+1}-U_{d,0}|^{3})+O(|V_{d+1}-V_{d,0}|^{3}),

where
f=f(x,y)
,
f_{1}=\partial f/\partial x
,
f_{2}=\partial f/\partial y
,
f_{11}=\partial^{2}f/\partial x^{2}
,
f_{22}=\partial^{2}f/\partial y^{2}
, and
f_{12}=\partial^{2}f/\partial x\partial y
.
By the proof of Proposition 8.2, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{f_{1}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(U_{d}-U_{d,0})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{f_{1}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(U_{d+1}-U_{d,0})\},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{f_{11}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(U_{d}-U_{d,0})^{2}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{f_{11}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(U_{d+1}-U_{d,0})^{2}\}.

Furthermore, for
l=k+(j-1)n
, we define

\displaystyle z_{d,0,l}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{d-1}\breve{a}_{i,k}\epsilon_{ij}+n^{-1/2}\sum\limits_{i=d+1}^{n}\breve{a}_{i,k}\xi_{ij},

\displaystyle z_{d,l}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle z_{d,0,l}+n^{-1/2}\breve{a}_{d,k}\xi_{dj},

\displaystyle z_{d+1,l}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle z_{d,0,l}+n^{-1/2}\breve{a}_{d,k}\epsilon_{dj},

and
{\boldsymbol{z}}_{d,0}=(z_{d,0,1},\dots,z_{d,0,np})^{\top}
,
{\boldsymbol{z}}_{d}=(z_{d,1},\dots,z_{d,np})^{\top}
. By Taylor’s expansion, we have

\displaystyle V_{d}-V_{d,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{l=1}^{np}\partial_{l}F_{\beta}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{d,0})(z_{d,l}-z_{d,0,l})+\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{l_{1}=1}^{np}\sum\limits_{l_{2}=1}^{np}\partial_{l_{1}}\partial_{l_{2}}F_{\beta}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{d,0})(z_{d,l_{1}}-z_{d,0,l_{1}})(z_{d,l_{2}}-z_{d,0,l_{2}})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}\sum\limits_{l_{1}=1}^{np}\sum\limits_{l_{2}=1}^{np}\sum\limits_{l_{3}=1}^{np}\partial_{l_{1}}\partial_{l_{2}}\partial_{l_{3}}F_{\beta}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{d,0}+\iota({\boldsymbol{z}}_{d}-{\boldsymbol{z}}_{d,0}))(z_{d,l_{1}}-z_{d,0,l_{1}})(z_{d,l_{2}}-z_{d,0,l_{2}})(z_{d,l_{3}}-z_{d,0,l_{3}}),

for some
\iota\in(0,1)
. Since
\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{i})=\mathbb{E}({\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i})=0
under
H_{0}
, and
\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{i}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{i}^{\top})=\mathbb{E}({\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}^{\top})
, it can be easily verified that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{z_{d,l}-z_{d,0,l}\mid\mathcal{F}_{d}\}=\mathbb{E}\{z_{d+1,l}-z_{d,0,l}\mid\mathcal{F}_{d}\},\quad\mathbb{E}\{(z_{d,l}-z_{d,0,l})^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{d}\}=\mathbb{E}\{(z_{d+1,l}-z_{d,0,l})^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{d}\}.

By Lemma A.2 in
Chernozhukov et al. 2013
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum\limits_{l_{1}=1}^{np}\sum\limits_{l_{2}=1}^{np}\sum\limits_{l_{3}=1}^{np}\partial_{l_{1}}\partial_{l_{2}}\partial_{l_{3}}F_{\beta}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{d,0}+\iota({\boldsymbol{z}}_{d}-{\boldsymbol{z}}_{d,0}))\bigg|\leq C\beta^{2}

By the definition of
\xi_{ij}
and
\epsilon_{ij}
,
i=1,\ldots,n,j=1,\ldots,p
and Assumption
3
,
\xi_{ij}
are Gaussian random variables and
\epsilon_{ij}
are sub-Gaussian random variables. Then we have
\mathrm{P}(\max\limits_{1\leq i\leq n,1\leq j\leq p}|\epsilon_{ij}|>C\mathrm{log}(np))\rightarrow 0
, and
\mathrm{P}(\max\limits_{1\leq i\leq n,1\leq j\leq p}|\xi_{ij}|>C\mathrm{log}(np))\rightarrow 0
.

Hence,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{6}\sum\limits_{l_{1}=1}^{np}\sum\limits_{l_{2}=1}^{np}\sum\limits_{l_{3}=1}^{np}\partial_{l_{1}}\partial_{l_{2}}\partial_{l_{3}}F_{\beta}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{d,0}+\iota({\boldsymbol{z}}_{d}-{\boldsymbol{z}}_{d,0}))(z_{d,l_{1}}-z_{d,0,l_{1}})(z_{d,l_{2}}-z_{d,0,l_{2}})(z_{d,l_{3}}-z_{d,0,l_{3}})\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\beta^{2}n^{-3/2}\{\mathrm{log}(np)\}^{3}

holds with probability approaching one. Consequently, with probability approaching one, we have

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}\{f_{2}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(V_{d}-V_{d,0})\}-\mathbb{E}\{f_{2}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(V_{d+1}-V_{d,0})\}|\leq C\beta^{2}n^{-3/2}\{\mathrm{log}(np)\}^{3}.

Similarly, it can be verified that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}\{f_{22}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(V_{d}-V_{d,0})^{2}\}-\mathbb{E}\{f_{22}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(V_{d+1}-V_{d,0})^{2}\}|\leq C\beta^{2}n^{-3/2}\{\mathrm{log}(np)\}^{3},

and

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}\{f_{12}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(U_{d}-U_{d,0})(V_{d}-V_{d,0})\}-\mathbb{E}\{f_{12}(U_{d,0},V_{d,0})(U_{d+1}-U_{d,0})(V_{d+1}-V_{d,0})\}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\beta^{2}n^{-3/2}\{\mathrm{log}(np)\}^{3}.

By Lemma A.2 in
Chernozhukov et al. 2013
and the expression of
V_{d}-V_{d,0}
, we have
\mathbb{E}(|V_{d}-V_{d,0}|^{3})=O\{n^{-3/2}\{\mathrm{log}(np)\}^{3}\}
.

Next, we consider
W_{d},W_{d+1}
and
W_{d,0}
. Define

\displaystyle z^{w}_{d,0,k}=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1}<\ell_{2}\leq d-1}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}\breve{a}_{\ell_{2},k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\ell_{1}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\ell_{2}}/\sqrt{2\text{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\sum_{d+1\leq\ell_{1}<\ell_{2}\leq n}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}\breve{a}_{\ell_{2},k}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{\ell_{1}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{\ell_{2}}/\sqrt{2\text{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}\sum_{\ell_{2}=d+1}^{k_{n}}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}\breve{a}_{\ell_{2},k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\ell_{1}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{\ell_{2}}/\sqrt{2\text{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})},

\displaystyle z^{w}_{d,k}=

\displaystyle z^{w}_{d,0,k}+n^{-1}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}\breve{a}_{d,k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\ell_{1}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}/\sqrt{2\text{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\sum_{\ell_{2}=d+1}^{n}\breve{a}_{d,k}\breve{a}_{\ell_{2},k}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{\ell_{2}}/\sqrt{2\text{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})},

\displaystyle z^{w}_{d+1,k}=

\displaystyle z^{w}_{d,0,k}+n^{-1}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}\breve{a}_{d,k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\ell_{1}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{d}/\sqrt{2\text{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\sum_{\ell_{2}=d+1}^{n}\breve{a}_{d,k}\breve{a}_{\ell_{2},k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{d}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{\ell_{2}}/\sqrt{2\text{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})},

and let
z^{w}_{d,0}=(z^{w}_{d,0,1},\ldots,z^{w}_{d,0,n})^{\top}
and
z^{w}_{d}=(z^{w}_{d,1},\ldots,z^{w}_{d,n})^{\top}
.
By Taylor’s expansion, we have

\displaystyle W_{d}-W_{d,0}=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}\partial_{l}F_{\beta}\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,0}\right)\left(z^{w}_{d,l}-z^{w}_{d,0,l}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\partial_{k}\partial_{l}F_{\beta}\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,0}\right)\left(z^{w}_{d,l}-z^{w}_{d,0,l}\right)\left(z^{w}_{d,k}-z^{w}_{d,0,k}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{v=1}^{n}\partial_{v}\partial_{k}\partial_{l}F_{\beta}\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,0}+\iota\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d}-\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,0}\right)\right)\left(z^{w}_{d,l}-z^{w}_{d,0,l}\right)\left(z^{w}_{d,k}-\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,0,k}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,v}-\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,0,v}\right),

for some
\iota\in(0,1)
. Again, due to
\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{i})=\mathbb{E}({\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i})=0
under
H_{0}
, and
\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{i}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{i}^{\top})=\mathbb{E}({\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}^{\top})
, we verify that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(z_{d,l}^{w}-z_{d,0,l}^{w}|\mathcal{F}_{d})=\mathbb{E}(z_{d+1,l}^{w}-z_{d,0,l}^{w}|\mathcal{F}_{d}),\quad\mathbb{E}\{(z_{d,l}^{w}-z_{d,0,l}^{w})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{d}\}=\mathbb{E}\{(z_{d+1,l}^{w}-z_{d,0,l}^{w})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{d}\}.

We next consider
\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq n}|z^{w}_{d,k}-z^{w}_{d,0,k}|\right)
with,

\displaystyle z^{w}_{d,k}-z^{w}_{d,0,k}=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}\breve{a}_{d,k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\ell_{1}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}/\sqrt{2\text{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}

(26)

\displaystyle\qquad+n^{-1}\sum_{\ell_{2}=d+1}^{n}\breve{a}_{d,k}\breve{a}_{\ell_{2},k}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{\ell_{2}}/\sqrt{2\text{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})},

(27)

Notice that, for Equation (
26
),

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}\breve{a}_{d,k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\ell_{1}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}/\sqrt{2\text{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}=n^{-1}\left\{\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\ell_{1}}\right\}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}\breve{a}_{d,k}/\sqrt{2\text{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})},

and by Assumption
1
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\ell_{1}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}b_{\ell}\boldsymbol{Z}_{\ell_{1}-\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{d-1}\left(\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}b_{\ell_{1}-\ell}\right)\boldsymbol{Z}_{\ell}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{d-1}c_{\ell,k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{\ell},

where
c_{\ell,k}=\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}b_{\ell_{1}-\ell}
and for notational convenience, we set
b_{\ell}=0
for
\ell<0
. And we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{d-1}c_{\ell,k}^{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{d-1}\left(\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}b_{\ell_{1}-\ell}\right)^{2}\leq\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{d-1}\left(\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}|b_{\ell_{1}-\ell}|\right)^{2}

(28)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{d-1}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}\sum_{\ell_{2}=1}^{d-1}|b_{\ell_{1}-\ell}||b_{\ell_{2}-\ell}|=\sum_{s=1}^{d-1}\sum_{t=1}^{d-1}\sum_{\ell=\max(s,t)+1}^{\mathrm{min}(s,t)+d-1}|b_{s}||b_{t}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(d-1\right)\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}|b_{\ell}|\right)^{2}.

Taking the expectation on
\{\boldsymbol{Z}_{\ell}\}_{d-1}
,

\displaystyle\phi^{2}_{z,d}:=

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left\{\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{d-1}\left(c_{\ell,k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{\ell}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\left\{\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{d-1}c_{\ell,k}^{2}\right\}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(d-1\right){\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d},

where the last inequality follows from the Equation (
28
) and Assumption
1
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle M_{z,d}:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\max_{1\leq k\leq n,\ell\leq d-1}\left(c_{\ell,k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{\ell}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\leq d-1}\mathbb{E}\left\{\max_{1\leq k\leq n}(c_{\ell,k}^{2})\left(\boldsymbol{Z}_{\ell}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\leq d-1}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}|b_{\ell_{1}-\ell}|\right)^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}_{\ell}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(d-1\right)\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}|b_{\ell}|\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}\{(\boldsymbol{Z}_{d-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d})^{2}\}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(d-1\right){\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}.

By Lemma
8.14
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\left|\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}\breve{a}_{d,k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\ell_{1}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\sqrt{(d-1){\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}\mathrm{log}p}\right\}+\mathbb{E}\left\{\sqrt{(d-1){\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}}\mathrm{log}p\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2n^{1/2}\mathrm{log}n\{\mathrm{tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\}^{1/2}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\left|n^{-1}\left\{\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\ell_{1}}\right\}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}\breve{a}_{d,k}/\sqrt{2\text{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}\right|\right]

(29)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left\{\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\left|\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{d-1}\breve{a}_{\ell_{1},k}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\ell_{1}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{d}\right|\right\}/\sqrt{2\text{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\mathrm{log}n.

Taking the same procedure for Equation (
27
) and combing the Equation (
29
), we have,
\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq n}|z^{w}_{d,k}-z^{w}_{d,0,k}|\right)\lesssim n^{-1/2}\mathrm{log}n
.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{6}\sum_{l,k,v=1}^{n}\partial_{v}\partial_{k}\partial_{l}F_{\beta}\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,0}+\iota\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d}-\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,0}\right)\right)\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,l}-\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,0,l}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,k}-\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,0,k}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,v}-\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,0,v}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\beta^{2}n^{-3/2}(\mathrm{log}n)^{3},

Similarly,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{6}\sum_{l,k,v=1}^{n}\partial_{v}\partial_{k}\partial_{l}F_{\beta}\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d+1,0}+\iota\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d+1}-\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,0}\right)\right)\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d+1,l}-\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,0,l}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d+1,k}-\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,0,k}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d+1,v}-\boldsymbol{z}^{w}_{d,0,v}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\beta^{2}n^{-3/2}(\mathrm{log}n)^{3},

hold with probability approaching one. Consequently, we obtain,

\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{f_{1}\left(W_{d,0},V_{d,0}\right)\left(W_{d}-W_{d,0}\right)\right\}-\mathbb{E}\left\{f_{1}\left(W_{d,0},V_{d,0}\right)\left(W_{d+1}-W_{d,0}\right)\right\}\right|\lesssim\beta^{2}n^{-3/2}(\mathrm{log}n)^{3},

\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{f_{2}\left(W_{d,0},V_{d,0}\right)\left(V_{d}-V_{d,0}\right)\right\}-\mathbb{E}\left\{f_{2}\left(W_{d,0},V_{d,0}\right)\left(V_{d+1}-V_{d,0}\right)\right\}\right|\lesssim\beta^{2}n^{-3/2}(\mathrm{log}n)^{3}.

Similarly, it can be verified that

\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{f_{11}\left(W_{d,0},V_{d,0}\right)\left(W_{d}-W_{d,0}\right)^{2}\right\}-\mathbb{E}\left\{f_{22}\left(W_{d,0},V_{d,0}\right)\left(W_{d+1}-W_{d,0}\right)^{2}\right\}\right|\lesssim\beta^{2}n^{-3/2}(\mathrm{log}n)^{3},

\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{f_{22}\left(W_{d,0},V_{d,0}\right)\left(V_{d}-V_{d,0}\right)^{2}\right\}-\mathbb{E}\left\{f_{22}\left(W_{d,0},V_{d,0}\right)\left(V_{d+1}-V_{d,0}\right)^{2}\right\}\right|\lesssim\beta^{2}n^{-3/2}\{\mathrm{log}(np)\}^{3},

and

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{f_{12}\left(W_{d,0},V_{d,0}\right)\left(W_{d}-W_{d,0}\right)\left(V_{d}-V_{d,0}\right)\right\}-\mathbb{E}\left\{f_{12}\left(W_{d,0},V_{d,0}\right)\left(W_{d+1}-W_{d,0}\right)\left(V_{d+1}-V_{d,0}\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\beta^{2}n^{-3/2}(\mathrm{log}n)^{3}.

Combining the above facts with the condition
p\lesssim n^{\nu}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{d=1}^{n}\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{f(U_{d},V_{d})\right\}-\mathbb{E}\left\{f(U_{d+1},V_{d+1})\right\}\right|\lesssim\beta^{2}n^{-1/2}(\mathrm{log}n)^{3}\rightarrow 0,

as
(n,p)\rightarrow\infty
.

8.7
Proof of Theorem
6

Proof.

It suffices to show the conclusion holds for Gaussian sequence
\{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Without loss of generality, we assume
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0}=\mathbf{0}
. Define
s_{i}=\mathbb{I}(i>\tau)
, we rewrite

\displaystyle W(k)^{(G)}

\displaystyle=g(k)\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{i,k}a_{j,k}(s_{i}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}+{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i})^{\top}(s_{j}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}+{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{j})

\displaystyle=g(k)\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{i,k}a_{j,k}({\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{j}+s_{i}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{j}+s_{j}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}+s_{i}s_{j}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}).

Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{W(k)^{(G)}\right\}=\mu_{M,k}+g(k)\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{i,k}a_{j,k}s_{i}s_{j}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}.

We only need to prove
g(k)\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{i,k}a_{j,k}s_{i}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{j}=o_{p}\{\sqrt{2p^{-1}\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}\}
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{var}\bigg[\bigg\{g(k)\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{i,k}a_{j,k}s_{i}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{j}\bigg\}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\{g(k)\}^{2}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}a_{i_{1},k}s_{i_{1}}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\bigg)^{\top}\bigg\{\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{n}a_{j_{1},k}a_{j_{2},k}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{M}({|j_{2}-j_{1}|})\bigg\}\bigg(\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}a_{i_{2},k}s_{i_{2}}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\{g(k)\}^{2}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}a_{i_{1},k}s_{i_{1}}\bigg)^{2}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}\bigg\{\sum_{h\in\mathcal{M}}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{n-h}a_{j_{1},k}a_{j_{1}+h,k}\mathit{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}_{M}({|h|})\bigg\}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{k^{4}(n-k)^{4}}{n^{6}p}\frac{n^{3}}{k^{2}(n-k)^{2}}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\bf\Omega}_{n}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\right)=O\left(np^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\bf\Omega}_{n}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\right)=o\{p^{-1}\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})\},

under
H_{1;n,p}
.

Thus, according to the proof of Theorem 1, under
H_{1;n,p}
, we have

\displaystyle~~~~\frac{W(k)^{(G)}-\mu_{M,k}}{\sqrt{2p^{-1}\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{W}(\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{1,k/n},\dots,\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{k_{n},k/n})+\frac{g(k)\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{i,k}a_{j,k}s_{i}s_{j}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}}{\sqrt{2p^{-1}\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{1,k/n},\dots,\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{k_{n},k/n})+\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}(\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{1,k/n},\dots,\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{k_{n},k/n})+\frac{g(k)\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{i,k}a_{j,k}s_{i}s_{j}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}}{\sqrt{2p^{-1}\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}}+o_{p}(1),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{1,k/n},\dots,\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{k_{n},k/n})

\displaystyle:=\frac{2g(k)}{\sqrt{2p^{-1}\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}}\sum\limits_{1\leq i<j\leq k_{n}}\sum\limits_{\ell_{1}=(i-1)w_{n}+1}^{iw_{n}-M}\sum\limits_{\ell_{2}=(j-1)w_{n}+1}^{jw_{n}-M}\sum\limits_{t\in\mathcal{A}}\eta_{\ell_{1},k/n,t}\eta_{\ell_{2},k/n,t},

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}(\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{1,k/n},\dots,\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{k_{n}.k/n})

\displaystyle:=\frac{2g(k)}{\sqrt{2p^{-1}\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}}\sum\limits_{1\leq i<j\leq k_{n}}\sum\limits_{\ell_{1}=(i-1)w_{n}+1}^{iw_{n}-M}\sum\limits_{\ell_{2}=(j-1)w_{n}+1}^{jw_{n}-M}\sum\limits_{t\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}\eta_{\ell_{1},k/n,t}\eta_{\ell_{2},k/n,t}.

According to the same arguments as in Theorem 5 in
Wang and Feng 2023
, we can rewrite

\displaystyle M_{n,p}=\max\left\{\max_{k=1,\ldots,n-1}\max_{t\in\mathcal{A}}|C_{0,t}(k)|,\max_{k=1,\ldots,n-1}\max_{t\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}|C_{0,t}(k)|\right\}.

Define
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i\mathcal{A}}:=(\xi_{it},t\in\mathcal{A})^{\top}
,
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}}:=(\xi_{it},t\in\mathcal{A}^{c})^{\top}
,

{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}}:=\mathrm{cov}({\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i\mathcal{A}},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i\mathcal{A}})
,
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathcal{A}^{c},\mathcal{A}^{c}}:=\mathrm{cov}({\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}})
, and
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}^{c}}:=\mathrm{cov}({\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i\mathcal{A}},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}})
. We decompose
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}}=\boldsymbol{U}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}}+\boldsymbol{V}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}}
, where
\boldsymbol{U}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}}:={\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathcal{A}^{c},\mathcal{A}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i\mathcal{A}}
, and

\boldsymbol{V}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}}:={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathcal{A}^{c},\mathcal{A}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i\mathcal{A}}
, which satisfy

\boldsymbol{U}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}}\sim N(\mathbf{0},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathcal{A}^{c},\mathcal{A}^{c}}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathcal{A}^{c},\mathcal{A}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}^{c}})
,

\boldsymbol{V}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}}\sim N(\mathbf{0},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathcal{A}^{c},\mathcal{A}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}^{c}})
,
\boldsymbol{U}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i\mathcal{A}}
are independent.
Based on Theorem
5
, we have known
\max_{k=1,\ldots,n-1}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}(\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{1,k/n},\dots,\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{k_{n},k/n})
is asymptotically independent of
\max\limits_{k=1,\ldots,n-1}\max\limits_{t\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}|C_{0,t}(k)|
. Hence, it suffices to prove
\max\limits_{k=1,\ldots,n-1}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}(\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{1,k/n},\dots,\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{k_{n},k/n})
is asymptotically independent of
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i\mathcal{A}}
.
We rewrite

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}(\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{1,k/n},\dots,\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{k_{n},k/n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2g(k)}{\sqrt{2p^{-1}\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}}\sum\limits_{1\leq i<j\leq k_{n}}\sum\limits_{\ell_{1}=(i-1)w_{n}+1}^{iw_{n}-M}\sum\limits_{\ell_{2}=(j-1)w_{n}+1}^{jw_{n}-M}a_{\ell_{1},k}a_{\ell_{2},k}(\boldsymbol{U}_{\ell_{1}\mathcal{A}^{c}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{\ell_{2}\mathcal{A}^{c}}+2\boldsymbol{U}_{\ell_{1}\mathcal{A}^{c}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}_{\ell_{2}\mathcal{A}^{c}}+\boldsymbol{V}_{\ell_{1}\mathcal{A}^{c}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}_{\ell_{2}\mathcal{A}^{c}}).

With similar discussion on Equation (
24
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}\bigg(\bigg|\max_{k=1,\dots,n-1}\frac{2g(k)}{\sqrt{2p^{-1}\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}}\sum\limits_{1\leq i<j\leq k_{n}}\sum\limits_{\ell_{1}=(i-1)w_{n}+1}^{iw_{n}-M}\sum\limits_{\ell_{2}=(j-1)w_{n}+1}^{jw_{n}-M}a_{\ell_{1},k}a_{\ell_{2},k}\boldsymbol{U}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}}\bigg|\geq\epsilon\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n\exp\bigg\{-C_{\epsilon}\sqrt{\frac{p}{|\mathcal{A}|}}\bigg\}\cdot\mathrm{log}n\rightarrow 0,

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}\bigg(\bigg|\max_{k=1,\dots,n-1}\frac{2g(k)}{\sqrt{2p^{-1}\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}}\sum\limits_{1\leq i<j\leq k_{n}}\sum\limits_{\ell_{1}=(i-1)w_{n}+1}^{iw_{n}-M}\sum\limits_{\ell_{2}=(j-1)w_{n}+1}^{jw_{n}-M}a_{\ell_{1},k}a_{\ell_{2},k}\boldsymbol{V}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}_{i\mathcal{A}^{c}}\bigg|\geq\epsilon\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n\exp\bigg\{-C_{\epsilon}\sqrt{\frac{p}{|\mathcal{A}|}}\bigg\}\cdot\mathrm{log}n\rightarrow 0,

due to
|\mathcal{A}|=o\{p/(\mathrm{log}n)^{2}\}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}(\tilde{\eta}_{1,k/n},\dots,\tilde{\eta}_{k_{n},k/n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2g(k)}{\sqrt{2p^{-1}\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})}}\sum\limits_{1\leq i<j\leq k_{n}}\sum\limits_{\ell_{1}=(i-1)w_{n}+1}^{iw_{n}-M}\sum\limits_{\ell_{2}=(j-1)w_{n}+1}^{jw_{n}-M}a_{\ell_{1},k}a_{\ell_{2},k}\boldsymbol{U}_{\ell_{1}\mathcal{A}^{c}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}_{\ell_{2}\mathcal{A}^{c}}+o_{p}(1).

It implies that
\max_{k=1,\ldots,n-1}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}(\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{1,k/n},\ldots,\tilde{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}_{k_{n},k/n})
is asymptotically independent of
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i\mathcal{A}}
with Lemma
8.15
.

8.8
Proof of Theorem
7

8.8.1
Consistency of
\hat{\tau}_{S}

To prove the consistency of
\hat{\tau}_{S}
, it suffices to show that, for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle n\mathrm{P}(|\hat{\tau}_{S}-\tau|>n\epsilon)\rightarrow 0.

Without loss of generality, we assume
\hat{\tau}_{S}<\tau
and define

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{j}(k)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left(S_{kj}-\frac{k}{n}S_{nj}\right),

then

\displaystyle\sqrt{p}\left\{W(k)-\mu_{k,M}\right\}=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\bigg\{\widetilde{C}^{2}_{j}(k)-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{h=0}^{M}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n-h}\{2-\mathbb{I}(h=0)\}\breve{a}_{i,k}\breve{a}_{i+h,k}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(h)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\bigg\}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\widetilde{C}^{2}_{j}(k)-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}H_{j}(k),

where

H_{j}(k)=n^{-1}\sum\nolimits_{h=0}^{M}\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n-h}\{2-\mathbb{I}(h=0)\}\breve{a}_{i,k}\breve{a}_{i+h,k}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\mathit{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(h)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}
and
\boldsymbol{e}_{j}
represents the
p
-dimensional unit vector with 1 in the
j
th entry and 0 elsewhere.
We will show that, for all
t
such that
t<\tau-n\epsilon
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}\bigg(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\widetilde{C}^{2}_{j}(t)-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}H_{j}(t)>\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\widetilde{C}^{2}_{j}(\tau)-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}H_{j}(\tau)\bigg)\rightarrow 0.

Observe that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\widetilde{C}^{2}_{j}(t)-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}H_{j}(t)-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\widetilde{C}^{2}_{j}(\tau)+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}H_{j}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\frac{1}{n}\left(S_{tj}-\frac{t}{n}S_{nj}\right)^{2}-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\frac{1}{n}\left(S_{\tau j}-\frac{\tau}{n}S_{nj}\right)^{2}-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}H_{j}(t)+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}H_{j}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle G_{1}+G_{2}.

Let
\widetilde{S}_{kj}=\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{ij}
, then

\displaystyle G_{1}=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\frac{1}{n}\left(S_{tj}-\frac{t}{n}S_{nj}\right)^{2}-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\frac{1}{n}\left(S_{\tau j}-\frac{\tau}{n}S_{nj}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\bigg\{\frac{1}{n}\left(\widetilde{S}_{tj}-\frac{t}{n}\widetilde{S}_{nj}-\frac{t(n-\tau)}{n}\delta_{j}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\left(\widetilde{S}_{\tau j}-\frac{\tau}{n}\widetilde{S}_{nj}-\frac{\tau(n-\tau)}{n}\delta_{j}\right)^{2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\frac{(t^{2}-\tau^{2})(n-\tau)^{2}}{n^{3}}\delta_{j}^{2}-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\bigg\{\frac{2t(n-\tau)}{n^{2}}\delta_{j}\left(\widetilde{S}_{tj}-\frac{t}{n}\widetilde{S}_{nj}\right)-\frac{2\tau(n-\tau)}{n^{2}}\delta_{j}\left(\widetilde{S}_{\tau j}-\frac{\tau}{n}\widetilde{S}_{nj}\right)\bigg\}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\bigg\{\frac{1}{n}\left(\widetilde{S}_{tj}-\frac{t}{n}\widetilde{S}_{nj}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\left(\widetilde{S}_{\tau j}-\frac{\tau}{n}\widetilde{S}_{nj}\right)^{2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle G_{11}+G_{12}+G_{13}.

Since
t<\tau-n\epsilon
, we have

\displaystyle G_{11}=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\frac{(t^{2}-\tau^{2})(n-\tau)^{2}}{n^{3}}\delta_{j}^{2}<-\frac{t+\tau}{n}n\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|^{2}\epsilon.

It remains to prove, for large enough constant
C>0
,

n\mathrm{P}(G_{12}>Cn\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|^{2}\epsilon)\rightarrow 0,

(30)

n\mathrm{P}(G_{13}+G_{2}>Cn\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|^{2}\epsilon)\rightarrow 0.

(31)

Observe that

\displaystyle G_{12}=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\bigg\{\frac{2t(n-\tau)}{n^{2}}\delta_{j}\left(\widetilde{S}_{tj}-\frac{t}{n}\widetilde{S}_{nj}\right)-\frac{2\tau(n-\tau)}{n^{2}}\delta_{j}\left(\widetilde{S}_{\tau j}-\frac{\tau}{n}\widetilde{S}_{nj}\right)\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\frac{2(n-\tau)}{n^{2}}\delta_{j}\bigg\{(\tau-t)\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t}\epsilon_{ij}+\tau\sum\limits_{i=t+1}^{\tau}\epsilon_{ij}-\frac{\tau^{2}-t^{2}}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{ij}\bigg\}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle G_{121}+G_{122}+G_{123}.

We first consider the term
G_{123}
,

\displaystyle n\mathrm{P}(G_{123}>Cn\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|^{2}\epsilon)=

\displaystyle n\mathrm{P}\bigg(\frac{2(n-\tau)(\tau^{2}-t^{2})}{n^{3}}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\delta_{j}\epsilon_{ij}>Cn\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|^{2}\epsilon\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\mathrm{P}\bigg(\frac{2(n-\tau)(\tau^{2}-t^{2})}{n^{3}}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\sum_{i=1-M}^{n}\omega_{i}\boldsymbol{Z}_{i}>Cn\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|^{2}\epsilon\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2n\frac{n\{6+\mathbb{E}(Z_{11}^{4})\}\{\lambda_{\max}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})\}^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|^{4}(\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}|b_{\ell}|)^{4}}{C^{4}n^{4}\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|^{8}\epsilon^{4}}\left\{\frac{2(n-\tau)(\tau^{2}-t^{2})}{n^{3}}\right\}^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|^{4}\epsilon^{4}}\rightarrow 0,

where the first inequality holds by Markov inequality,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\delta_{j}\epsilon_{ij}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\left\{\sum_{s=1-M}^{0}(\sum_{\ell=1-s}^{M}b_{\ell})\boldsymbol{Z}_{s}+\sum_{s=1}^{n-M}(\sum_{\ell=0}^{M}b_{\ell})\boldsymbol{Z}_{s}+\sum_{s=n-M+1}^{n}(\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-s}b_{\ell})\boldsymbol{Z}_{s}\right\}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\sum_{s=1-M}^{n}\omega_{s}\boldsymbol{Z}_{s},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{({\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{Z}_{i})^{4}\right\}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p}({\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2})_{j}^{4}\mathbb{E}(Z_{ij}^{4})+6\sum_{1\leq j\neq\ell\leq p}({\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2})_{j}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2})_{\ell}^{2}\mathbb{E}(Z_{ij}^{2})\mathbb{E}(Z_{i\ell}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z_{i1}^{4})\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{p}({\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2})_{j}^{2}\right\}^{2}+6\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{p}({\boldsymbol{\delta}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2})_{j}^{2}\right\}^{2},

where
|\omega_{s}|\leq\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}|b_{\ell}|<\infty
by Assumption
1
for all
s=1-M,\ldots,n
and
(\cdot)_{j}
represents the
j
th entry of a vector. Similarly, we can show that
n\mathrm{P}(G_{121}>Cn\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|^{2}\epsilon)\rightarrow 0
and
n\mathrm{P}(G_{122}>Cn\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|^{2}\epsilon)\rightarrow 0
.

We next consider the term
G_{13}+G_{2}
. Notice that, by Theorem
2
, we have,
\mathbb{E}(G_{13})-G_{2}\rightarrow 0
. Taking the same procedure as
Wang et al. 2019
and replace the Lemma A.1 by Markov’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle n\mathrm{P}(G_{13}+G_{2}>Cn\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|^{2}\epsilon)\lesssim n\frac{n\{\lambda_{\max}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})\}^{2}(\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}|b_{\ell}|)^{4}}{n^{4}\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|^{4}\epsilon^{2}}+o(1)\rightarrow 0.

Consequently, the Equation (
30
) and (
31
) hold, which complete the proof.

8.8.2
Consistency of
\hat{\tau}
or
\hat{\tau}^{\dagger}

According to Theorem 6 in
Wang and Feng 2023
, both
\hat{\tau}_{M}
and
\hat{\tau}_{M}^{\dagger}
are consistent, that is,
(\hat{\tau}_{M}-\tau)/n=o_{p}(1)
and
(\hat{\tau}_{M}^{\dagger}-\tau)/n=o_{p}(1)
. We next examine the consistency of
\hat{\tau}
and
\hat{\tau}^{\dagger}
under the following three scenarios:
(i)
\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|_{\infty}<c\sqrt{\mathrm{log}(np)/n}
for some constant
c>0
, while
n\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|_{2}^{2}\to\infty
;
(ii)
\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|_{\infty}/\sqrt{\mathrm{log}(np)/n}\to\infty
, while
n\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|_{2}^{2}<c
for some constant
c>0
; and
(iii) both
\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|_{\infty}/\sqrt{\mathrm{log}(np)/n}\to\infty
and
n\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|_{2}^{2}\to\infty
.

For Case (i), Theorem
2.1
implies that
p_{S_{n,p}}\to 0
, while
p_{M^{\dagger}{n,p}}\not\to 0
. Hence,
\Pr(p_{T_{ms}}<p_{M^{\dagger}_{n,p}})\to 1
. Combined with the consistency result above, it follows that
(\hat{\tau}^{\dagger}-\tau)/n=o_{p}(1)
. A similar argument shows that
\hat{\tau}^{\dagger}
is also consistent under Case (ii). For Case (iii), the result follows immediately since at least one of the estimators
\hat{\tau}_{S}
,
\hat{\tau}_{M}
, or
\hat{\tau}_{M^{\dagger}}
is consistent.

8.9
Proof of the uniform consistency of the long-run variance estimator

We first restate some notations and statistics in
Chan 2022
. Let
\sigma_{j}=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}n\mathrm{var}(\bar{X}_{ij})=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\gamma_{k,j}
, where
\gamma_{k,j}=\mathrm{cov}(X_{0,j},X_{k,j})
. The
m
th order difference-based estimator of
\sigma_{j}
is

\hat{\sigma}_{j,(m)}=\sum_{|k|<\ell_{j}}K(k/\ell_{j})\hat{\gamma}_{k,j}^{D},

where
K
is a kernel and

\hat{\gamma}_{k,j}^{D}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=mh+|k|+1}^{n}D_{i,j}D_{i-|k|,j},\\

and the
m
th order lag-
h
difference statistics are

D_{i,j}=\sum_{s=0}^{m}d_{s}X_{i-sh,j},~~~~i=mh+1,\ldots,n,d_{0}+\ldots+d_{m}=0,d_{0}^{2}+\ldots,d_{m}^{2}=1.

A
SSUMPTION
6

(Assumption 5 in
Chan 2022
,Near-origin property) The kernel
K
satisfies that there exists
\tilde{q}\in\mathbb{N}
and
B\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
such that
\{K(t)-K(0)\}/|t|^{\tilde{q}}\rightarrow B
as
t\rightarrow 0
.

L
EMMA
8.10

Suppose the Assumption 1 and 5(Assumption
6
) in
Chan 2022
and Assumption
1
–
2
hold and there exists
0<\kappa_{q}<3
, such that
\tilde{q}=3-\kappa_{q}
,
\nu\lesssim\mathrm{min}\{q/2-2,\tilde{q}q/(1+2\tilde{q})-1\}
and
\|{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\|_{\infty}=O(1)
. For
\delta_{n}=n^{-\tilde{\delta}}
,
\tilde{\delta}>0
sufficiently small, it holds that

\mathrm{P}(\max_{1\leq h\leq p}|\hat{\sigma}_{h}-\sigma_{h}|\geq\delta_{n}^{2})\lesssim n^{-C},~~~~C>0,

as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Proof.

We follow the proof of Lemma E.5 in
Jirak 2015
. It follows that for large enough
p
and
n
, we have,

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}(\max_{1\leq h\leq p}|\hat{\sigma}_{h}^{2}-\sigma_{h}^{2}|\geq\delta_{n}^{2}\mathrm{inf}_{h}^{*}\sigma_{h})\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{p}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{h}}\mathrm{P}(|\hat{\gamma}_{j,h}^{D}-\gamma_{j,h}|\geq\delta_{n}^{2}\mathrm{inf}_{h}^{*}\sigma_{h}/(2\ell_{h}))

(32)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\delta_{n}^{2}\mathrm{inf}_{h}^{*}\sigma_{h})^{-p/2}\sum_{h=1}^{p}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{h}}(2\ell_{h})^{q/2}\|\hat{\gamma}_{j,h}^{D}-\gamma_{j,h}\|_{q/2}^{q/2}.

Let
u_{\tilde{q}}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|k|^{\tilde{q}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}b_{\ell}b_{\ell+k}
. By Assumption
1
and Lemma
8.13
, we have

u_{\tilde{q}}\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|k|^{\tilde{q}}\sum_{\ell=k}^{\infty}|b_{\ell}|\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}o(|k|^{\tilde{q}-4})=O(1).

We repeat the Lemma B.1 in
Chan 2022
and acquire the results
\sqrt{n}\max_{1\leq h\leq p}\|\hat{\gamma}_{j,h}^{D}-\gamma_{j,h}\|_{q/2}=O(1)
for
j=o(n)
. From the Theorem 4.1 in
Chan 2022
, we see that the optimal
\ell_{h}
has the same optimal rate
\ell_{h}\asymp n^{1/(1+2\tilde{q})}
where
\tilde{q}=3-\kappa_{q}
,
\kappa_{q}>0
. Thus, the Equation (
32
) is bounded by
pn^{2\tilde{\delta}}n^{(q/2+1)/(1+2\tilde{q})}n^{-q/2}\lesssim n^{-C}
. Using that
|\hat{\sigma}_{h}-\sigma_{h}|\leq(\mathrm{inf}_{h}^{*}\sigma_{h})^{-1}|\hat{\sigma}_{h}^{2}-\sigma_{h}^{2}|
, the proof is completed.

8.10
Some Useful Lemmas

L
EMMA
8.11

Suppose
\{z_{tj};t=1,\dots,n,j=1,\dots,p\}
are independent and identically distributed random variables satisfying
\mathbb{E}(z_{tj})=0
,
\mathbb{E}(z_{tj}^{2})=1
and
\mathbb{E}(z_{tj}^{4})=\mu_{4}<\infty
. Let
{\boldsymbol{w}}:=\sum\nolimits_{t=0}^{\infty}a_{t}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{t}
, where
{\boldsymbol{z}}_{t}=(z_{t1},\dots,z_{tp})^{\top}
and
\sum\nolimits_{t=0}^{\infty}|a_{t}|<\infty
.
Then, there exists a positive constant
\tau_{1}\leq 3
such that for all
p\times p
positive semi-definite matrix
{\bf B}
,

\mathbb{E}\{({\boldsymbol{w}}^{\top}{\bf B}{\boldsymbol{w}})^{2}\}\leq\tau_{1}\{\mathbb{E}({\boldsymbol{w}}^{\top}{\bf B}{\boldsymbol{w}})\}^{2}.

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}({\boldsymbol{w}}^{\top}{\bf B}{\boldsymbol{w}})=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}b_{jj}\mathbb{E}\{\sum\limits_{t=0}^{\infty}a_{t}^{2}z_{ti}^{2}\}=\sum\limits_{t=0}^{\infty}a_{t}^{2}\mathrm{tr}({\bf B}).

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{({\boldsymbol{w}}^{\top}{\bf B}{\boldsymbol{w}})^{2}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}=1}^{p}b_{j_{1}j_{2}}b_{j_{3}j_{4}}\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\prod\limits_{s=1}^{4}\bigg(\sum\limits_{t=0}^{\infty}a_{t}z_{t,j_{s}}\bigg)\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}b_{jj}^{2}\bigg(\sum\limits_{t=0}^{\infty}a_{t}^{4}\mu_{4}+3\sum\limits_{t_{1}\neq t_{2}\geq 0}a_{t_{1}}^{2}a_{t_{2}}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{j_{1}\neq j_{2}=1}^{p}(2b_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{2}+b_{j_{1}j_{1}}b_{j_{2}j_{2}})\bigg(\sum\limits_{t=0}^{\infty}a_{t}^{2}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}^{2}({\bf B})\bigg(\sum\limits_{t=0}^{\infty}a_{t}^{4}\mu_{4}+3\sum\limits_{t_{1}\neq t_{2}\geq 0}a_{t_{1}}^{2}a_{t_{2}}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\{2\mathrm{tr}({\bf B}^{2})+\mathrm{tr}^{2}({\bf B})\}\bigg(\sum\limits_{t=0}^{\infty}a_{t}^{2}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(6+\mu_{4})\bigg(\sum\limits_{t=0}^{\infty}a_{t}^{2}\bigg)^{2}\mathrm{tr}^{2}({\bf B}).

Taking
\tau_{1}=6+\mu_{4}
, the lemma has been proved.

L
EMMA
8.12

Under Assumptions
1
–
2
, for any
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, there exists a constant
\tau_{2}
such that

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\big[\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k_{1}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k_{2}}-\mathrm{tr}\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(|k_{1}-k_{2}|\}\big]\big[\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k_{3}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k_{4}}-\mathrm{tr}\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(|k_{3}-k_{4}|\}\big]\right|\leq\tau_{2}\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2}),

(33)

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{k_{1}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{k_{2}}-\mathrm{tr}\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{M}(|k_{1}-k_{2}|\}\big]\big[\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{k_{3}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{k_{4}}-\mathrm{tr}\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{M}(|k_{3}-k_{4}|\}\big]\big|\leq\tau_{2}\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n,M}^{2}).

(34)

Proof.

Following Hölder inequality,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\big[\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k_{1}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k_{2}}-\mathrm{tr}\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(|k_{1}-k_{2}|\}\big]\big[\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k_{3}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k_{4}}-\mathrm{tr}\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(|k_{3}-k_{4}|\}\big]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}\big[\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k_{1}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k_{2}}-\mathrm{tr}\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(|k_{1}-k_{2}|\})\big]^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k_{3}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k_{4}}-\mathrm{tr}\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(|k_{3}-k_{4}|\}\big]^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

It suffice to prove that, for all
h>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{h}-\mathrm{tr}\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(h)\}\big]^{2}\leq\tau_{2}\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2}).

Recall
\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{h}={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\sum\nolimits_{k=0}^{\infty}b_{k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{h-k}
, we split
\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{h}
into two independent parts as

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{h}={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}b_{k+h}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k}+{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\sum\limits_{k=0}^{h-1}b_{k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{h-k}:=\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{h,1}+\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{h,2}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{h,1}-\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{h})\big\}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\{\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}b_{k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}b_{k+h}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k}-\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{h})\big\}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\{\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}b_{k}b_{k+h}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k}-\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{h})+\sum\limits_{k_{1}\neq k_{2}}^{\infty}b_{k_{1}}b_{k_{2}+h}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k_{1}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k_{2}}\big\}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\{\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}b_{k}b_{k+h}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k}-\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{h})\big\}^{2}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big\{\big(\sum\limits_{k_{1}\neq k_{2}}^{\infty}b_{k_{1}}b_{k_{2}+h}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k_{1}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k_{2}}\big)^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}b_{k}^{2}b_{k+h}^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\{\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k}-\mathrm{tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})\big\}^{2}\Big]+\sum\limits_{k_{1}\neq k_{2}}^{\infty}b_{k_{1}}^{2}b_{k_{2}+h}^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big\{\big(\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k_{1}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k_{2}}\big)^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}\neq k_{2}}^{\infty}b_{k_{1}}b_{k_{2}+h}b_{k_{2}}b_{k_{1}+h}\mathbb{E}\Big\{\big(\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k_{1}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k_{2}}\big)\big(\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k_{2}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k_{1}}\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big\{\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}b_{k}^{2}b_{k+h}^{2}(\mu_{4}+1)+\Big(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}b_{k}^{2}\Big)\Big(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}b_{k+h}^{2}\Big)+\Big(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}b_{k}b_{k+h}\Big)^{2}\Big\}\mathrm{tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\mu_{4}+3)\Big(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}b_{k}^{2}\Big)^{2}\mathrm{tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big\{\big(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{h,2}\big)^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big\{\Big(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}b_{k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{-k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\sum\limits_{k=0}^{h-1}b_{k}\boldsymbol{Z}_{h-k}\Big)^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}b_{k}^{2}\Big)\Big(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{h-1}b_{k}^{2}\Big)\mathrm{tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}).

Equation (
33
) is hold with
\mathrm{tr}({\bf\Omega}_{n}^{2})=\sum_{h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathcal{N}}(1-\frac{|h_{1}|}{n})(1-\frac{|h_{2}|}{n})a_{h_{1}}a_{h_{2}}\mathrm{tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})
.
Equation (
34
) is guaranteed by repeating the above prove with
b_{k,M}=b_{k}\mathbb{I}_{\{k\leq M\}}
.

L
EMMA
8.13

\{b_{n},n\geq 1\}
is a sequence of numbers. Suppose that there exist a constant
k>1
such that
b_{n}=o(n^{-k})
, then

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{m=n}^{\infty}b_{m}=o(n^{-(k-1)}).

Proof.

There exist
N\in\mathbb{N}
, such that for all
n\geq N,n^{k}b_{n}\leq 1
. Then, for all
n\geq N
,

\displaystyle n^{k-1}\sum\limits_{m=n}^{\infty}b_{m}=\sum\limits_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{t=mn}^{(m+1)n-1}n^{k}b_{t}=o(n^{-(k-1)}).

L
EMMA
8.14

Let
{\boldsymbol{X}}_{1}^{(0)},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{X}}_{n}^{(0)}
be independent centered random vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{p}
with
p\geq 2
, and denote
{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i}^{(0)}=(X_{i1}^{(0)},\ldots,X_{ip}^{(0)})^{\top}
. Define
Z=\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{ij}^{(0)}\right|
,
M=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\left|X_{ij}^{(0)}\right|
and
\sigma^{2}=\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\{(X_{ij}^{(0)})^{2}\}
. Then,

\mathbb{E}(Z)\lesssim K\left\{\sigma\sqrt{\mathrm{log}p}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(M^{2})}\mathrm{log}p\right\}.

Proof.

See Lemma E.1 in
Chernozhukov et al. 2017
.

L
EMMA
8.15

Let
\{(U,U_{p},\tilde{U}_{p})\in\mathbb{R}^{3};p\geq 1\}
and
\{(V,V_{p},\tilde{V}_{p})\in\mathbb{R}^{3};p\geq 1\}
be two sequences of random variables with

U_{p}\rightarrow U
and
V_{p}\rightarrow V
in distribution as
p\rightarrow\infty
. Assume
U
and
V
are continuous random variables and that

\displaystyle\tilde{U}_{p}=U_{p}+o_{p}(1)~{\textrm{and}}~\tilde{V}_{p}=V_{p}+o_{p}(1).

If
U_{p}
and
V_{p}
are asymptotically independent, then
\tilde{U}_{p}
and
\tilde{V}_{p}
are also asymptotically independent.

Proof.

See Lemma 7.10 in
Feng et al. 2024
.
